<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that there are absolute constants <i>c</i> &gt; 0 and <i>K</i><sub>0</sub> such that $`W_r(k)\gt k^{ck\lfloor\log_2 r\rfloor}`$ for every $`k\ge K_0`$ and <i>r</i> ≥ 2. Consequently $`W_r(k)^{1/k}\to\infty`$ for each fixed <i>r</i> ≥ 2, giving a quantitative positive resolution of Erdős's superexponential-growth question, including the two-color case.

- **claim-superexp-lower-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-main-intro)): There is an absolute integer $K_0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge K_0$ and every integer $r\ge2$, $$W_r(k)>k^{c k\lfloor\log_2r\rfloor}, \qquad c=10^{-5}.$$
  - Explanation: A single absolute threshold on the progression length gives a superexponential lower bound for the van der Waerden number, uniformly in the number of colors at least two.
- **claim-dilation-short-periods** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-dilation)): We have $\log\lambda\le h_0\log(2M)$. If $1\le h\le2M$, then every prime-power divisor of $h$ with prime at most $h_0$ divides $\lambda$. Consequently, $h\mid\lambda$ whenever $h\le h_0$, and, for every integer $t$, the denominator $$\label{setup:denominator} s=\frac{h}{\gcd(h,\lambda t)}$$ is either $1$ or greater than $h_0$.
  - Explanation: The dilation is not too large, and it absorbs every small-prime part of every short period, so a reduced denominator is either trivial or large.
- **claim-prime-window** (theorem, [source](#stmt-setup-prime)): For all sufficiently large integers $k$, there is a prime $P$ satisfying $k<P\le2k^2$.
  - Explanation: A prime between the progression length and twice its square exists, shown by comparing the size of the central binomial coefficient with its possible small prime factors.
- **claim-mesh-width** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mesh-width)): For every $I\in V$ and every $Y\in\overline I$, $$\label{mesh:width-bounds} \frac14H\bigl(\rho(Y)+\alpha\bigr) \le \|I\|\le2H\bigl(\rho(Y)+\alpha\bigr)\le2H.$$ Moreover, $\|V\|=2m=O(k^3\log k)$.
  - Explanation: Each interval of the adaptive mesh has width comparable to its distance from the cut plus a small scale, and the mesh has few intervals.
- **claim-mesh-neighbors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mesh-neighbors)): For $I\in V$, let $w=\|I\|$ and $$N(I)=\{Y\in\mathbb T:\operatorname{dist}_{\mathbb T}(Y,\overline I)\le w\}.$$ Every $J\in V$ whose closure meets $N(I)$ satisfies $\|J\|\ge w/16$. The arc $N(I)$ contains at most $49$ mesh endpoints.
  - Explanation: Mesh intervals near a given interval are not much finer than it, and its neighborhood contains few mesh endpoints.
- **claim-mesh-truncated** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mesh-truncated)): On every circle arc of length at most $4H$, the output $V_\eta$ is constant between at most $16004D$ breakpoints. Its value at a breakpoint is also determined, and need not equal either neighboring value.
  - Explanation: After truncating near the cut, the second-system label changes only boundedly often along a short arc.
- **claim-arrangement-signs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-count-arrangement)): Let $s$ affine hyperplanes be given in $\mathbb R^e$, and record, for each hyperplane, the sign in $\{-,0,+\}$ of a defining affine function. The number of realized joint sign vectors is at most $$\label{count:arrangement-bound} \sum_{d=0}^e\binom{s}{d}\sum_{j=0}^{e-d}\binom{s}{j} \le(e+1)^2(s+1)^e.$$ The same bound applies when the parameter space is restricted to an arbitrary subset of $\mathbb R^e$.
  - Explanation: The number of joint sign vectors, equality included, cut out by affine hyperplanes is polynomial in their number for fixed dimension.
- **claim-global-label-words** (theorem, [source](#stmt-count-global)): Let $T_{\mathrm{glob}}$ be the number of words $\bigl(L(n_j)\bigr)_{0\le j<k}$ arising from the progressions in [setup:cyclic](#setup:cyclic). Then $$\label{count:global-bound} \log T_{\mathrm{glob}} =O\bigl(D(\log k+\log\lambda)\bigr) =O(k^{3/10}\log k)=o(M).$$
  - Explanation: The logarithm of the number of full-label words along progressions is small compared with the multiplicity scale.
- **claim-local-patterns** (theorem, [source](#stmt-count-local)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for every $h_0<h\le2M$ and every full label $\beta\in\mathcal L$, the number of pairs consisting of $t\in\{0,\ldots,h-1\}^D$ and an eligible pattern for $h,t$ containing $\beta$ is at most $$\label{count:local-bound} (CDh)^{6D}.$$ The constant is independent of $q$, $\lambda$, $\beta$, and $D$.
  - Explanation: Eligible local patterns through a prescribed label are counted by a bound independent of the dilation, the modulus and the width of the anchor interval.
- **claim-asymmetric-lll** (theorem, [source](#stmt-outer-lll)): Let $\mathcal E$ be a finite family of events. Suppose a graph on $\mathcal E$, with neighbor sets $\Gamma(E)$, has the property that each $E$ is independent of the sigma-algebra generated by the events outside $\{E\}\cup\Gamma(E)$. If numbers $z_E\in(0,1)$ satisfy $$\label{outer:lll-condition} \mathbb P(E)\le z_E\prod_{F\in\Gamma(E)}(1-z_F) \qquad(E\in\mathcal E),$$ then $\mathbb P(\bigcap_{E\in\mathcal E}E^c)>0$.
  - Explanation: The finite asymmetric local lemma: weights satisfying the product condition guarantee that with positive probability no bad event occurs.
- **claim-balanced-outer-coloring** (construction, [source](#stmt-outer-coloring)): For all sufficiently large $k$, there is a map $c_*:\mathcal L\to\{0,1\}$ with the following properties.* 1. *In every cyclic progression having at least $\delta k$ light positions, each bit occurs on at least one quarter of its light positions when labels are colored by $c_*$.* 2. *In every eligible recorded pattern of [count:eligible](#count:eligible), each bit occurs on at least one quarter of its regular positions.* *The threshold on $k$ is absolute.
  - Explanation: A coloring of the label space exists that balances both bits on light positions of rich progressions and on regular positions of eligible patterns.
- **claim-affinity-small-box** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dichotomy-affinity)): Let $z_j=a+jb$ in $\mathbb T^e$, for $0\le j<k$, and let $J\subseteq\{0,\ldots,k-1\}$. Suppose real representatives $Z_j\in\mathbb R^e$, $j\in J$, lie in one box whose coordinate intervals have length at most $2H$. If $2kH<1$, there are $A,B\in\mathbb R^e$ such that $Z_j=A+jB$ for every $j\in J$.
  - Explanation: Samples of a torus progression lying in a small box have real representatives that are affine in the index.
- **claim-closest-return-drift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dichotomy-drift)): Choose a heavy label and let $J$ be its set of occurrence indices, with $m_*=\|J\|>k/M$. Let $h$ be the smallest positive difference between two members of $J$, attained at $j'$ and $j'+h$. Set $$u=\hat x(n_{j'+h})-\hat x(n_{j'}),\qquad v=\tilde y(n_{j'+h})-\tilde y(n_{j'}).$$ Then, for sufficiently large $k$, $$\label{dichotomy:drift-bounds} \begin{gathered} 1\le h\le2M,\\ \|u_i\|\le\frac{H_x}{m_*-1}\le\frac{2MH_x}{k},\qquad \|v_i\|\le\frac{2H}{m_*-1}\le\frac{4MH}{k} \quad(1\le i\le D), \end{gathered}$$ and $v=\lambda u$ as real vectors.
  - Explanation: The closest return of a heavy label gives a bounded period and a tiny drift, linked by the dilation.
- **claim-rational-path** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dichotomy-path)): With $h,u,v$ as in [dichotomy:drift](#dichotomy:drift), there is $t\in\{0,\ldots,h-1\}^D$ such that $$\label{dichotomy:path-equations} \begin{aligned} x(n_j)&=x(n_0)+\frac jh(t+u),\\ y(n_j)&=y(n_0)+\frac jh(\lambda t+v) \end{aligned} \qquad\text{in }\mathbb T^D\quad(0\le j<k).$$ Moreover $\gcd(t_1,\ldots,t_D,h)=1$, and indices incongruent modulo $h$ have distinct full labels. Consequently, all occurrences of any one full label lie in a single residue class modulo $h$.
  - Explanation: The progression follows a rational path of the closest-return period, residues modulo that period have distinct labels, and equal labels share a residue class.
- **claim-heavy-interval-drift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dichotomy-heavy-width)): For every heavy index $j_*$ of the fixed progression and each $1\le i\le D$, $$\label{dichotomy:relative-width} \|v_i\|\le\frac{2M}{k}\,\|V(y_i(n_{j_*}))\|.$$ The heavy label here need not be the one used to choose $h$.
  - Explanation: The drift is small relative to the width of the interval of every heavy index, not only the one that chose the period.
- **claim-outer-dichotomy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-dichotomy-main)): For every sufficiently large $k$, the coloring $c_0$ from [outer:pullback](#outer:pullback) has the following property. Every cyclic progression $n_j=n_0+jd$, $0\le j<k$, with $d\ne0$ in $G$, satisfies at least one of these alternatives:* 1. *each bit occurs among $c_0(n_0),\ldots,c_0(n_{k-1})$ at least $\gamma k$ times;* 2. *there are $A,B\in\mathbb R^D$ such that $\tilde y(n_j)=A+jB$ for all $0\le j<k$.* *The threshold on $k$ is absolute.
  - Explanation: Every nonzero-step cyclic progression either contains many of each outer bit or has centered representatives affine in the index.
- **claim-key-multiplicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perturb-multiplicity)): For every progression $n_j=n_0+jd$ in $G$ with $d\ne0$ and $0\le j<k$, every key occurs at most four times among the $\kappa(n_j)$.
  - Explanation: Along a nonconstant progression, points scaled to be mutually separated meet a thin norm band in few places, so a key repeats only a bounded number of times.
- **claim-affine-signature-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perturb-signatures)): Let $T_{\mathrm{aff}}$ be the number of signatures realized by affine cyclic progressions, and let $T_{\mathrm{glob}}$ be the full-label word count of [count:global](#count:global). There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\label{perturb:signature-count} T_{\mathrm{aff}} \le C T_{\mathrm{glob}}\bigl(k(Dq^2+2)\bigr)^3.$$ In particular, $$\label{perturb:entropy} \log\max\{1,T_{\mathrm{aff}}\} \le O(k^{3/10}\log k)+6\log q+O(\log k) =O(k^{9/10}\log k)=o(pk).$$ All implied constants are absolute.
  - Explanation: Affine progressions realize few signatures, since the norm band indices are governed by three real coefficients; the entropy is small compared with the perturbation scale.
- **claim-cyclic-two-coloring** (theorem, [source](#stmt-perturb-cyclic)): There is an absolute integer $K_0$ such that, for every integer $k\ge K_0$, the construction in [sec:setup](#sec:setup) gives an integer $$N=q^D\ge k^{ck},\qquad c=10^{-5},$$ and a coloring $C:\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z\to\{0,1\}$ for which no progression $n_0,n_0+d,\ldots,n_0+(k-1)d$ with $d\ne0$ in $\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$ is monochromatic.
  - Explanation: A sparse random flip of the outer coloring, keyed by boxes and norm bands, yields a two-coloring of a large cyclic group with no monochromatic progression of nonzero step.
- **claim-digit-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-product)): Let $N,k\ge2$ be integers. Suppose that a coloring $C:\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z\to\{0,1\}$ has no monochromatic progression $a,a+d,\ldots,a+(k-1)d$ with $d\ne0$ in $\mathbb Z/N\mathbb Z$. Then, for every positive integer $m$, $$W_{2^m}(k)>N^m.$$ Consequently $W_r(k)>N^{\lfloor\log_2r\rfloor}$ for every integer $r\ge2$.
  - Explanation: Taking the same cyclic coloring in every base-N digit transfers a progression-free cyclic two-coloring to intervals and to all numbers of colors.
- **claim-large-color-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-large-r)): For every pair of integers $r\ge256$ and $k\ge3$, $$W_r(k)>\exp\!\left(\frac{(\log r)^2}{64\log2}\right).$$
  - Explanation: A digit-and-sphere coloring gives a superpolynomial lower bound in the number of colors, uniform in the progression length.
- **claim-uniform-growth-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-transfer-limits)): With integer parameters in both infima, $$\lim_{k\to\infty} \inf_{\substack{r\in\mathbb Z\\r\ge2}} \frac{\log W_r(k)}{k\log r}=\infty, \qquad \lim_{r\to\infty} \inf_{\substack{k\in\mathbb Z\\k\ge3}} \frac{\log W_r(k)}{\log r}=\infty.$$ In particular, $W_r(k)^{1/k}\to\infty$ for every fixed integer $r\ge2$, and $W_r(k)/r^A\to\infty$ as $r\to\infty$ for every fixed integer $k\ge3$ and every fixed real $A>0$.
  - Explanation: The two lower bounds yield uniform infinite limits, hence superexponential growth in the length and superpolynomial growth in the number of colors.
- **claim-recursive-upper-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-upper-finite)): Define $F(r,1)=1$ and $F(r,2)=r+1$ for every integer $r\ge1$. For $k\ge3$, with $r$ and $k$ fixed, define $$m_0=1,\qquad m_{t+1}=2m_tF(r^{m_t},k-1)\quad(0\le t<r),\qquad F(r,k)=m_r. \label{upper:recursion}$$ Then, for all positive integers $r,k$, $$W_r(k)\le F(r,k)<\infty.$$ The boundary parameter values are exactly $$W_r(1)=1,\qquad W_r(2)=r+1,\qquad W_1(k)=k.$$
  - Explanation: A block-and-focus recursion bounds the van der Waerden number, proving finiteness, and the boundary values are exact.

          "source_id": "PM-160.1"
        }
      ],

        "source_id": "PM-160.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-160.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **van der Waerden number W_r(k)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For positive integers $r$ and $k$, let $W_r(k)$ be the least positive integer $N$ such that every map $[N]\to[r]$ is constant on some arithmetic progression $$a,a+d,\ldots,a+(k-1)d,\qquad a,d\ge1,\quad a+(k-1)d\le N.$$
- **Eligible local patterns** ([source](#stmt-count-eligible)): Choose $x,y\in\mathbb T^D$ and $u,v\in\mathbb R^D$, and form $$\label{count:local-paths} X_z=x+\frac z h(t+u),\qquad Y_z=y+\frac z h(\lambda t+v)\qquad(0\le z<h) \quad\text{in }\mathbb T^D.$$ A position $z$ is *regular* if $\rho(Y_{z,i})\ge\eta$ in every rotating coordinate; if there are no rotating coordinates, every position is regular. Record $L(X_z,Y_z)$ at a regular position and a dummy symbol $*$ otherwise. The resulting word in $(\mathcal L\cup\{*\})^h$ is *eligible for $h,t$* if it admits a realization satisfying all of the following: 1. At least $h/2$ positions are regular, and the full labels at those positions are pairwise distinct. 2. $\|u_i\|\le H_x$ for every $i$, and $\|v_i\|\le H$ in every rotating coordinate. 3. In every stationary coordinate $i$, one has $\|v_i\|\le \|V(Y_{z,i})\|$ at every regular position $z$. The recorded word is the pattern; different realizations of one word do not define additional patterns.

| q-truncated-breakpoints | breakpoints of the truncated output | ≤ | $16004D$ | arcs of length at most 4H |

| q-digit-product-bound | W_{2^m}(k) | > | $N^m$ | cyclic coloring without monochromatic progressions |
| q-large-r-lower-bound | lower bound for W_r(k) in the number of colors | > | $\exp\!\left(\frac{(\log r)^2}{64\log2}\right)$ | explicit and uniform in k |
| q-two-term-value | W_r(2) | = | $r+1$ | k equal to two |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-prime** [claim](#stmt-setup-prime) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-prime): Compare the size of the central binomial coefficient with its prime valuations. -> A prime above k and below twice k squared.
- **step-dilation** [claim](#stmt-setup-dilation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-setup-dilation): Primary parts of short periods divide the dilation. -> Reduced denominators are trivial or large.
- **step-mesh-width** [claim](#stmt-mesh-width) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mesh-width): Exponential mesh and Lipschitz distance to the cut. -> Width bounds for mesh intervals.
- **step-mesh-neighbors** [claim](#stmt-mesh-neighbors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mesh-neighbors): Apply the width bound across the neighborhood. -> Uniform neighbor widths and endpoint bound.
- **step-mesh-truncated** [claim](#stmt-mesh-truncated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mesh-truncated): Count mesh endpoints outside the truncation zone. -> Bounded breakpoints on short arcs.
- **step-arrangement** [claim](#stmt-count-arrangement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-arrangement): Induction on hyperplanes and dimension, then zero sets. -> Bound on realized sign vectors.
- **step-global-words** [claim](#stmt-count-global) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-global): Two-parameter arrangements in each scalar coordinate. -> Bound on global label words.
- **step-local-patterns** [claim](#stmt-count-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-count-local): Anchor at one visit and count affine comparisons per coordinate. -> Bound on anchored eligible patterns.
- **step-lll** [claim](#stmt-outer-lll) / [proof](#proof-stmt-outer-lll): Induction on conditioned event sets. -> Positive probability of avoiding all events.
- **step-outer-coloring** [claim](#stmt-outer-coloring) / [proof](#proof-stmt-outer-coloring): Random bits, exponential tails and the local lemma. -> Balanced coloring of the labels.
- **step-affinity** [claim](#stmt-dichotomy-affinity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dichotomy-affinity): Integer second differences vanish in a small box. -> Affine representatives.
- **step-drift** [claim](#stmt-dichotomy-drift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dichotomy-drift): Smallest gap among heavy occurrences. -> Period and drift bounds.
- **step-path** [claim](#stmt-dichotomy-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dichotomy-path): Minimality of the gap and convexity of label boxes. -> Rational path and separated residues.
- **step-heavy-width** [claim](#stmt-dichotomy-heavy-width) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dichotomy-heavy-width): Compare extreme visits with the rational path. -> Drift relative to heavy interval widths.
- **step-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-dichotomy-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-dichotomy-main): Split into short and long periods; use dilation or eligible blocks. -> Bit-rich or affine progression.
- **step-key-multiplicity** [claim](#stmt-perturb-multiplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perturb-multiplicity): Separation of scaled points and thin norm band geometry. -> Bounded key multiplicity.
- **step-signatures** [claim](#stmt-perturb-signatures) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perturb-signatures): Three-parameter arrangement for the quadratic norm. -> Bound on affine signatures.
- **step-cyclic-coloring** [claim](#stmt-perturb-cyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-perturb-cyclic): Random flips per key and union bounds over both cases. -> Cyclic two-coloring without monochromatic progressions.
- **step-digit-product** [claim](#stmt-transfer-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-product): Base-N digit product with the first nonzero difference digit. -> Interval colorings for all color counts.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-main-intro) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-product-2): Combine the cyclic coloring with the digit product. -> Superexponential lower bound.
- **step-large-r** [claim](#stmt-transfer-large-r) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-large-r): Digit halves and squared norm exclude three-term progressions. -> Lower bound growing with the number of colors.
- **step-limits** [claim](#stmt-transfer-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-transfer-limits): Apply both lower bounds to the infima. -> Uniform infinite limits.
- **step-upper-recursion** [claim](#stmt-upper-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-finite): Nested induction on length and on foci with distinct colors. -> Recursive finite upper bound.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-no-mono-3ap-digit-sphere**: For the digit-half and squared-norm coloring of the integers 0 to b^s-1, with b the integer fourth root of r and s the number of whole factors of 16 in r, no nonconstant three-term progression is monochromatic. Hence the construction yields W_r(k) > b^s for every k at least 3.
- **inv-color-count-at-most-r**: The coloring that records the half of each digit together with the sum of squared digits uses at most r distinct colors, so it is a valid r-coloring.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-small-r-or-vague-threshold**: Apply the explicit lower bound in the number of colors to a color count below the stated threshold. -> The digit construction needs b and s to be positive with b at least 2, which is only guaranteed in the stated range of r.
- **anti-claim-matching-upper-bound**: Treat the explicit lower bound as a sharp or uniform estimate of the growth in the number of colors. -> The source asserts no uniform dependence on the progression length and no matching logarithmic exponent for the upper bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The coloring in the proof of the large-r lower bound is an explicit finite construction: digits in base b, half-membership bits and the sum of squared digits. It can be built and checked exhaustively for small r.

```python
# INPUTS: one integer r >= 256 on stdin.
# PRECONDITIONS: 256 <= r <= 10000 so that the interval of b^s integers is small enough to be checked exhaustively.
# OUTPUTS: line 1 holds b and s; line 2 holds one color token per integer n in 0..b^s-1, as half-bits then a dot then the sum of squared digits.
# TIME: O(b^s * s) arithmetic operations.
# SPACE: O(b^s * s) characters for the output.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers only; no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed input raises an exception and the run is a crash.
import sys
r = int(sys.stdin.read().split()[0])
b = 1
while (b + 1) ** 4 <= r:
    b += 1
s = 0
while 16 ** (s + 1) <= r:
    s += 1
half = (b + 1) // 2
out = []
for n in range(b ** s):
    digs = []
    m = n
    for i in range(s):
        digs.append(m % b)
        m //= b
    bits = ''.join('1' if u >= half else '0' for u in digs)
    out.append(bits + '.' + str(sum(u * u for u in digs)))
print(b, s)
print(' '.join(out))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The existence of a progression-free two-coloring of a cyclic group comes from a random choice resolved by a local-lemma argument. The source gives no procedure for producing the coloring, so there is nothing to implement as a library. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no agents, rules, state updates or simulated dynamics. It proves lower bounds for van der Waerden numbers, which gives no game or simulation mechanic. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper offers no checker, generator or developer utility. The elementary recursion for the upper bound is a definition used to prove finiteness, not a packaged tool. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only explicit finite recursion is the finiteness bound in the appendix. The central results (local lemma, random perturbation and union bounds) are existential and give no search procedure or stated complexity. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Labels, keys and signatures are proof-side words used for counting. No structure with operations, update costs or a query interface is described. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No parallel kernel, memory layout or circuit is discussed. The material is asymptotic existence mathematics with no hardware-facing quantity. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-threshold-absolute-k** (hypothesis): The main lower bound holds only for k at or above an absolute threshold K_0 that the paper does not make explicit, and with the tiny constant c. Blocked use: ['Reading the bound as a concrete lower bound for W_r(k) at small or moderate k', 'Quoting a numerical value of K_0']
- **lim-lower-bound-only** (formal_scope): The paper proves lower bounds on W_r(k) in the number of colors, and no uniform dependence on k or matching logarithmic exponent is asserted for the upper bound. Blocked use: ['Treating the explicit lower bound in r as a sharp estimate', 'Claiming a uniform upper bound in k for the growth in the number of colors']
- **lim-large-r-range** (hypothesis): The digit-and-sphere bound requires at least 256 colors and progression length at least three; it says nothing for fewer colors. Blocked use: ['Applying the explicit bound to r below 256', 'Using it for progression length below three']
- **lim-nonconstructive-coloring** (nonconstructive): The cyclic two-coloring is obtained by a local-lemma choice of label bits and then a random choice of flips, so it is an existence result with no stated procedure to produce the coloring. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem as an algorithm that outputs an explicit progression-free coloring', 'Inferring efficient search from the existence bounds']
- **lim-nonzero-step-cyclic** (edge_case): The cyclic coloring only avoids progressions with nonzero step in the cyclic group; in the digit product the coloring must have no monochromatic progression with nonzero step. Blocked use: ['Applying the cyclic coloring to degenerate constant progressions', 'Applying the product to a coloring that only avoids progressions of positive integer step in an interval']
- **lim-upper-recursion-coarse** (approximation): The recursive upper bound is a coarse form of the classical block-and-focus argument and proves finiteness; it is not a competitive quantitative bound. Blocked use: ['Using F(r,k) as a practical estimate of W_r(k)', 'Comparing it with primitive-recursive or Gowers-type bounds as if sharp']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 160 (`lean/docs/160.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Quantitative Superexponential Bounds for van der Waerden Numbers](../../preprints/Quantitative-Superexponential-Bounds-for-van-der-Waerden-Numbers-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Let $W(r,k)$ be the least interval length forcing a monochromatic $k$-term arithmetic progression in every coloring with at most $r$ colors. The formalized result gives an absolute threshold $K$ such that $W(r,k)>k^{k\lfloor\log_2 r\rfloor/100000}$ for all $k\ge K$ and $r\ge2$. It also establishes the associated growth limits, finiteness, and boundary values. The sharper intermediate estimates used in the paper are not part of the described formalization.

Comparator source `QuantitativeVanDerWaerden.lean` (943 bytes, sha256 `1caa563bbf7a5602adb8cdbbc06aaf1e3d4d5fe27ed4bebf2bf44cf2937a6593`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

universe u

namespace QuantitativeVanDerWaerden

def MonoAP {α : Type u} (c : ℕ → α) (k a d : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ j < k, c (a + j * d) = c a

def HasMonoAP {α : Type u} (c : ℕ → α) (k N : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ a d, 0 < d ∧ a + (k - 1) * d < N ∧ MonoAP c k a d

def IsRamsey (α : Type u) (k N : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ c : ℕ → α, HasMonoAP c k N

/-- The least positive Ramsey interval size. Statements about its Ramsey
property require a finiteness proof; no such proof is built into this definition. -/
noncomputable def W (r k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  sInf {N : ℕ | 0 < N ∧ IsRamsey (Fin r) k N}

open Filter

/-- An absolute quantitative lower bound, uniform over all color counts. -/
theorem uniform_lower_bound :
    ∃ K : ℕ, ∀ k ≥ K, ∀ r ≥ 2,
      (k : ℝ) ^ ((1 / 100000 : ℝ) * k * (Nat.log 2 r : ℝ)) < (W r k : ℝ) := by
  sorry

end QuantitativeVanDerWaerden

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that there are absolute constants <i>c</i> &gt; 0 and <i>K</i><sub>0</sub> such that $`W_r(k)\gt k^{ck\lfloor\log_2 r\rfloor}`$ for every $`k\ge K_0`$ and <i>r</i> ≥ 2. Consequently $`W_r(k)^{1/k}\to\infty`$ for each fixed <i>r</i> ≥ 2, giving a quantitative positive resolution of Erdős's superexponential-growth question, including the two-color case.
